Native cap1 consumed; existing Danus TASKs unchanged; ZERO formal/numerical allowance; original target OPEN.", "evidence": "# Joint candidate: sparse exact lifts and simplex universality for finite GD words\n\n**JOINT UNVERIFIED THEOREM CANDIDATE.** Analytical result of the ONE fresh\nbounded allocation in this directory. The sparse main-authored mechanism\nsurvives independent checking, and the proposed simplex extension admits the\nexplicit proof below. Neither result is a verifier-accepted project fact.\nThe original exact Zhang--Jiang minimax target remains OPEN.\n\nThe complete frozen source was `MAIN_CANDIDATE_UNVERIFIED.md`, 6752 bytes,\nactual SHA256\n`cd09d624628958d04d5728ca7fd6b0e8fa489ea8c72021d2d3151b8331870963`.\nIt was read completely and was not edited. The earlier epigraph audit is not\nused as mathematical authority; the needed elementary facts are derived here.\n\n## 1. Strongest conclusion established by this analytical argument\n\nFix an integer `n>=0` and a finite word `h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1})>=0`. Let\n\n    S = sum_{t<n} h_t,\n    p = #{t<n : h_t>0}.\n\nNormalize smoothness to one. Let `W_n(h)` be the original unrestricted\nworst-case normalized terminal objective. Define `W_n^simplex(h)` by\nrestricting its functions to\n\n    F_D(q) = max_{v in D} { <q,v>-||v||^2/2 },\n\nwhere, in some Euclidean dimension `m` with `1<=m<=p+1`,\n\n    D = conv{0,w_1,...,w_m},\n\nand `w_1,...,w_m` are linearly independent. Thus `D` is a genuine\nfull-dimensional `m`-simplex and zero is one of its vertices. Keep arbitrary\ninitial points and the complete actual minimum set, with the original choice\nof minimizer in the denominator. The candidate conclusion is\n\n    W_n^simplex(h) = W_n(h).                        (1.1)\n\nEquivalently, every positive-score original finite run can be approximated\nfrom below in normalized terminal score by such a simplex function under\nthe SAME unmodified GD word. The new run can be normalized by its OWN closest\nminimizer. There are at most `p+2<=n+2` vertices, in dimension at most\n`p+1<=n+1`, independently of the original ambient dimension.\n\nMore precisely, for a given strict-smoothness run, `m` can be taken to be its\nnumber of distinct nonzero sampled gradients, including the terminal sample.\nThe construction gives exactly `m+1` simplex vertices and dimension `m`.\nNo claim that this dimension bound is universally minimal is made.\n\nFor an arbitrary original 1-smooth run, first use `c f` with `c<1` close to\none. For that strict-smoothness run, the pre-compression lift reproduces all\nORIGINAL HORIZONTAL ITERATES exactly and has an explicit value correction.\nCompression preserves the lifted values and GD dynamics; it need not retain\nthe original horizontal coordinates as a distinguished coordinate block.\n\nEquation (1.1) is equality of SUPREMA. It is not finite-parameter exact\nrepresentation of an arbitrary 1-smooth run or attainment within the simplex\nclass. It neither chooses an adverse original run from `h` nor compares the\nscore with `U_n^ZJ`. At general smoothness `L`, the restricted class is\n`L F_D`, with step `h_i/L`.\n\n## 2. Elementary projection facts and the complete minimum set\n\nFor a nonempty compact convex set `D`, the maximizer defining `F_D` is unique:\ncompleting the square identifies it with the unique nearest point `P_D(q)`.\nFor `v in D`, the exact characterization is\n\n    P_D(q)=v  iff  <q-v,w-v><=0 for every w in D.     (2.1)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating the squared distance on the segment\nfrom `v` to `w` at zero. For sufficiency, expand\n\n    ||q-w||^2 = ||q-v||^2 + ||w-v||^2\n                -2<q-v,w-v> >= ||q-v||^2.\n\nFor a finite convex hull it is enough to check every generating point,\nbecause the left side of (2.1) is affine in `w`.\n\nThe two normal inequalities for `v=P_D(q)` and `v'=P_D(q')` imply\n\n    <q-q',v-v'> >= ||v-v'||^2.\n\nThus the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the affine maxima at each\nother's optimizers gives\n\n    0 <= F_D(q')-F_D(q)-<P_D(q),q'-q>\n      <= <P_D(q')-P_D(q),q'-q> <= ||q'-q||^2.\n\nConsequently `F_D` is differentiable with gradient `P_D` and is globally\n1-smooth. It is convex as a maximum of affine functions of `q` and finite\nbecause `D` is compact.\n\nWhen `0 in D`, the minimum is zero and its COMPLETE minimum set is the cone\n\n    C_D = { a : <a,v><=0 for every v in D }.          (2.2)\n\nMembership makes every defining expression nonpositive, while `v=0` gives\nzero. Conversely, if `<a,v>>0` for some `v in D`, then `theta v in D` for\n`0<=theta<=1`, and\n\n    <a,theta v>-||theta v||^2/2 > 0\n\nfor sufficiently small positive `theta`. Thus `a` is not a minimizer.\nThe polar here always means the cone with threshold ZERO, not a body polar\nwith threshold one. It is nonempty, closed, and convex, so every point has\na closest point in it. Existence follows, for example, by restricting a\ndistance-minimizing sequence to a bounded ball and taking a convergent\nsubsequence.\n\n## 3. Strict smoothness supplies the necessary strict rows\n\nLet `0<ell<1`, let `f` be convex differentiable and ell-smooth, and translate\nand subtract its chosen optimum so that `f(0)=min f=0`. Use its ACTUAL GD\nsamples `i=0,...,n`, including the terminal sample, with\n\n    x_{i+1}=x_i-h_i g_i,\n    g_i=grad f(x_i),  f_i=f(x_i).\n\nAdd a reference label `*` with `x_*=g_*=f_*=0`. Define\n\n    Q_ji = f_j-f_i+<g_j,x_i-x_j>+||g_j-g_i||^2/2,\n    gamma = (1-ell)/(2ell)>0.\n\nThe ell-smooth interpolation necessity is\n\n    Q_ji <= -gamma ||g_j-g_i||^2.                   (3.1)\n\nThis is the smoothness-scaled necessity part of accepted local fact\n`1252b8823bb5a290`; none of its duality or attainment conclusions is used.\nOne can also derive the precise orientation directly: the convex function\n`k(x)=f(x)-<g_j,x>` has a minimum at `x_j`, and an ell-smooth descent step\nfrom `x_i` of length `1/ell` gives\n\n    k(x_j) <= k(x_i-(g_i-g_j)/ell)\n             <= k(x_i)-||g_i-g_j||^2/(2ell).\n\nRearrange and add the `||g_i-g_j||^2/2` term to obtain (3.1).\n\nPut\n\n    eta_i = <x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\n    H_i = sum_{t<i} h_t eta_t,\n    eta_* = 0.\n\nThe optimum row `Q_{i,*}=-eta_i` gives\n\n    eta_i >= gamma ||g_i||^2 >= 0,                 (3.2)\n\nand `Q_{*,i}=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2` gives\n\n    f_i-||g_i||^2/2 >= gamma ||g_i||^2.             (3.3)\n\nIn particular every nonzero gradient has STRICTLY positive `eta_i`.\nIf `g_i=0`, convexity makes `x_i` a minimizer, so `f_i=eta_i=0`.\nAll later gradients and positions then remain zero-gradient/stationary.\n\nIf `g_i=g_j=g`, the two elementary convexity inequalities force\n\n    f_i-f_j=<g,x_i-x_j>.\n\nTherefore `eta_i=eta_j`, and direct substitution gives `Q_ji=0`.\nThe same conclusion applies to equal zero gradients and to the reference\nlabel. Distinct positions with the same gradient need not be identified as\npositions; only their gradient/height vertex labels will be identified.\n\n## 4. Mechanism 1: exact sparse normal-guard construction\n\nFor `R>0`, define\n\n    v_i=(g_i,eta_i/R),  v_*=0,\n    D_R=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n},\n    z_i=(x_i,-R-H_i/R),\n    a_R=(0,-R).\n\nThis is a compact finite polytope in one extra coordinate, with at most\n`n+2` generating points. The point `a_R` belongs to its full minimum cone\nbecause `<a_R,v_i>=-eta_i<=0`. For `z_0=(x_0,-R)`,\n\n    ||z_0-a_R||=||x_0||.                           (4.1)\n\nThe claimed identity is exact. For every physical sample `i` and every\n`j in {*,0,...,n}`,\n\n    <z_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>\n      = <x_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>-(eta_j-eta_i)\n        -(H_i+eta_i)(eta_j-eta_i)/R^2\n      = Q_ji-(H_i+eta_i)(eta_j-eta_i)/R^2.          (4.2)\n\nFor clarity, the middle terms expand to\n\n    <x_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>-(eta_j-eta_i)\n      = f_j-f_i+<g_j,x_i-x_j>+||g_j-g_i||^2/2.\n\nThis checks both the orientation `Q_ji` and the sign of the vertical\ncorrection. No position associated with the reference `*` appears in the\nnormal test other than through the zero jet in `Q_*i`.\n\nIf `g_i=g_j`, both terms in (4.2) vanish. Otherwise `-Q_ji>0` by (3.1).\nDefine the data-dependent finite number\n\n    A = max_{i=0,...,n; j in {*,0,...,n}; g_i!=g_j}\n          (H_i+eta_i)(eta_i-eta_j)_+ / (-Q_ji),      (4.3)\n\nwith maximum over an empty set equal to zero. Finiteness follows from\nfinitely many finite data and strictly positive denominators. Here\n`H_i+eta_i>=0` uses the nonnegative steps.\n\nFor `R^2>=A`, all expressions in (4.2) are nonpositive. By (2.1),\n\n    P_{D_R}(z_i)=v_i,  for EVERY physical i=0,...,n. (4.4)\n\nThe prospective positions satisfy\n\n    z_{i+1}=z_i-h_i v_i,                            (4.5)\n\nbecause `H_{i+1}=H_i+h_i eta_i`. Equations (4.4)--(4.5), by induction from\nthe stated initial point, prove that these are the ACTUAL ordinary GD\niterates on the single fixed function `F_{D_R}`. All original horizontal\npositions are reproduced exactly. The added coordinate moves by\n`-h_i eta_i/R`; it is not held fixed.\n\nThe value at every actual sample is also exact:\n\n    F_{D_R}(z_i)\n      = <z_i,v_i>-||v_i||^2/2\n      = f_i-C_i/R^2,\n    C_i = eta_i H_i+eta_i^2/2 >= 0.                (4.6)\n\nThe optimum/reference row is included in the guard; it is not left to a\nheuristic value estimate. If `g_i!=0`, (4.4) already implies positive value\nvia `F_{D_R}(z_i)>=||v_i||^2/2>0`. Taking `R` larger also makes the explicit\ncorrection in (4.6) as small as desired.\n\nThis completes Mechanism 1 with no gap found. If only `R^2=A` is used, some\ndistinct-vertex rows may be equalities; that boundary is insufficient for\nthe generic perturbation proposal. The next section deliberately chooses\nstrict quantitative slack instead.\n\n## 5. Quantitative strict margins, including zero-gradient samples\n\nSuppose at least one sampled gradient is nonzero. List the distinct nonzero\ngradient values as `g^1,...,g^m`, with their well-defined heights\n`eta^1,...,eta^m`. Write\n\n    g^0=0,  eta^0=0,\n    a(i) in {0,...,m},\n    g_i=g^{a(i)},  eta_i=eta^{a(i)}.\n\nThe equality of heights proved above makes this label map unambiguous,\nincluding repeated gradients at different physical times. Let\n\n    delta = min_{0<=a<b<=m} ||g^a-g^b|| > 0.        (5.1)\n\nChoose\n\n    R^2 >= max(1,2A).                              (5.2)\n\nFor a physical `i` and a target label `b`, define\n\n    N_{ib}=<z_i-v^{a(i)},v^b-v^{a(i)}>,\n    v^b=(g^b,eta^b/R).\n\nWhen `a(i)!=b`, choose any original representative of `b` (or the reference\nlabel for zero) in (4.2). The possible positive correction is at most\n`A(-Q_ji)/R^2<=(-Q_ji)/2`. Thus\n\n    N_{ib} <= Q_ji/2\n            <= -(gamma/2)||g_i-g^b||^2\n            <= -gamma delta^2/2.                  (5.3)\n\nFor equal labels it is identically zero. This retains every PHYSICAL\nsample row, even when one vertex label occurs repeatedly; those samples\nhave different `H_i` and must not be silently collapsed as time indices.\n\nIf `g_i=0` and `b!=0`, the exact formula is even more transparent:\n\n    N_{ib}=Q_{ji}-H_i eta^b/R^2<0.\n\nSo zero-gradient samples supply strict inequalities against every nonzero\nvertex. Their equality against zero itself is a harmless identical-vertex\nrow, not a missing strictness condition.\n\nThe strict margins also show that each distinct nonzero `v^b` is an exposed\nvertex of `D_R`: at an occurrence of label `b`, the vector `z_i-v^b`\nstrictly exposes it among all other generating points. Zero is exposed by\n`a_R`, since all `eta^b>0`. Thus, under (5.2), the sparse body already has\nexactly `m+1` vertices, although they need not be affinely independent.\n\n## 6. Mechanism 2: an explicit simplex perturbation with exact dynamics\n\nAppend `m` new mutually orthogonal coordinates. Let `e_1,...,e_m` be their\nstandard basis and set `e_0=0`. For a number `epsilon>0`, define\n\n    w^b=(v^b,epsilon e_b),  0<=b<=m,\n    D_{R,epsilon}=conv{0,w^1,...,w^m}.\n\nIn particular `w^0=0`; a zero-gradient vertex is NOT given a new nonzero\ncomponent. Every occurrence of the same nonzero gradient receives exactly\nthe same component. Define cumulative chronological vectors\n\n    b_i=sum_{t<i} h_t e_{a(t)},\n    Z_i=(z_i,-epsilon b_i),\n    A_R=(a_R,0).\n\nThe INITIAL appended coordinates are zero. Their subsequent values above\nare recomputed from all the actual preceding steps, rather than assumed to\nstay zero. By construction,\n\n    Z_0=(x_0,-R,0),\n    Z_{i+1}=Z_i-h_i w^{a(i)}.                       (6.1)\n\nThe normal condition has an explicit perturbation identity. With\n`a=a(i)`, for every target label `b`,\n\n    <Z_i-w^a,w^b-w^a>\n       = N_{ib}\n         -epsilon^2 <b_i+e_a,e_b-e_a>.             (6.2)\n\nThere is no term linear in `epsilon`, because old and new coordinate blocks\nare orthogonal and the new initial block is zero.\n\nLet `b_{i,a}=<b_i,e_a>` for `a>0`, and take `b_{i,0}=0`. All these numbers\nare nonnegative and at most `S`. The coefficient of `epsilon^2` in (6.2)\nis exactly\n\n    0                         if a=b;\n    b_{i,a}-b_{i,b}+1          if a,b>0 and a!=b;\n    b_{i,a}+1                  if a>0 and b=0;\n    -b_{i,b}                   if a=0 and b>0.\n\nHence it is at most `S+1`. Equal-label rows remain exactly zero for every\nphysical occurrence. At zero-gradient samples the perturbation only makes\nthe distinct-vertex normal inequality more negative.\n\nChoose, in addition to (5.2),\n\n    0<epsilon^2 <= gamma delta^2/[4(S+1)].          (6.3)\n\nCombining (5.3) and (6.2) gives, for all different labels,\n\n    <Z_i-w^{a(i)},w^b-w^{a(i)}>\n         <= -gamma delta^2/4 < 0.                 (6.4)\n\nThe identical-label rows are zero. Thus every generator satisfies the normal\ncondition, and (2.1) proves\n\n    P_{D_{R,epsilon}}(Z_i)=w^{a(i)}\n\nat every physical index, including the terminal. Along with (6.1), this\nproves that `Z_i` is the ACTUAL GD run of `F_{D_{R,epsilon}}` under the same\nword. This is a quantitative proof of the proposal, not an appeal to\ngenericity or continuity at a supposedly strict equality.\n\nThe vectors `w^1,...,w^m` are linearly independent: projection of a linear\nrelation to the new coordinate block gives `epsilon` times every coefficient\nequal to zero. Therefore this body is a genuine `m`-simplex in its span,\nwith exactly `m+1` vertices including zero.\n\nThe nearby minimum remains exact:\n\n    <A_R,w^b>=-eta^b<0 for b>0,\n    ||Z_0-A_R||=||x_0||.                           (6.5)\n\nThus the whole minimum cone has not been replaced by the origin, and no\nlarge artificial coordinate is paid for in the initial distance.\n\nFinally, every perturbed value has the explicit formula\n\n    F_{D_{R,epsilon}}(Z_i)\n      = f_i-C_i/R^2-epsilon^2 beta_i,\n    beta_i=<b_i,e_{a(i)}>+||e_{a(i)}||^2/2.         (6.6)\n\nIf `a(i)=0`, both `C_i` and `beta_i` are zero and the value is exactly zero.\nOtherwise\n\n    beta_i=b_{i,a(i)}+1/2,\n    0<beta_i<=S+1/2.\n\nThis proves value continuity quantitatively while preserving the original\nstrict-run horizontal trajectory exactly before compression.\n\nFor any desired strict-run terminal value loss `tau>0`, the simultaneous\nexplicit choices\n\n    R^2 >= max(1,2A,2C_n/tau),\n    0<epsilon^2 <= min{\n        gamma delta^2/[4(S+1)],\n        tau/[2(S+1/2)]\n    }                                              (6.7)\n\nmake the total loss in (6.6) at most `tau`, while all different-label normal\nguards remain strictly negative. Every bound is finite and strictly\npositive where a positive perturbation is needed. No uniform lower bound\non `delta`, or uniform upper bound on `A`, is assumed.\n\n## 7. Compression to exactly m dimensions and the own closest radius\n\nThe construction so far lives in the original space plus `1+m` coordinates.\nLet\n\n    E=span{w^1,...,w^m},  dim E=m,\n\nand let `P_E` be orthogonal projection onto `E`. Since the entire body lies\nin `E`, for every `Z` and every `w in D_{R,epsilon}`,\n\n    <Z,w>=<P_E Z,w>,\n    ||Z-w||^2=||P_E Z-w||^2+||Z-P_E Z||^2.\n\nConsequently both the value and the selected projection gradient are\npreserved:\n\n    F_D(Z)=F_D(P_E Z),\n    P_D(Z)=P_D(P_E Z).\n\nPut `q_i=P_E Z_i` and `a=P_E A_R`. Then\n\n    q_{i+1}=q_i-h_i w^{a(i)},\n    P_D(q_i)=w^{a(i)},\n    F_D(q_i)=F_D(Z_i),\n    <a,w^b>=-eta^b<=0.\n\nThus the compressed points are an actual GD run of the same simplex\nfunction regarded as a function on the `m`-dimensional Euclidean space\n`E`; choosing an orthonormal basis identifies it with `R^m`. The point `a`\nis a true minimizer, and\n\n    ||q_0-a||=||P_E(Z_0-A_R)||<=||x_0||.            (7.1)\n\nThis handles the selected radius. The OWN closest radius is preserved\nexactly under the compression as well. Writing `C_E` for the polar cone\ninside `E`, the ambient minimum set is\n\n    C_D = C_E + E^perp.\n\nOrthogonal decomposition therefore gives\n\n    dist(Z_0,C_D)=dist(P_E Z_0,C_E).                 (7.2)\n\nIf the terminal value is positive, neither starting point is a minimizer:\na zero gradient would leave every following iterate fixed with zero value.\nThe shared radius in (7.2), denoted `rho`, is then positive, and (7.1) gives\n\n    0<rho<=||x_0||.\n\nFor `r=||x_0||>0`, if `tau<f_n`, (6.7) consequently yields\n\n    2 F_D(q_n)/rho^2 >= 2(f_n-tau)/r^2.             (7.3)\n\nThe positivity of the lower numerator is explicit; no inequality with a\nnegative numerator is reversed by changing the denominator. Neither the\nselected compressed radius nor the closest radius is asserted to equal the\noriginal `r`. The inequality is sufficient.\n\nThe number of distinct sampled positions is at most `p+1`, because a zero\nstep leaves the position unchanged and each positive step can add at most\none position. Therefore\n\n    m <= p+1,\n    number of simplex vertices = m+1 <= p+2.\n\nThis count includes the terminal gradient. Repeated gradients can reduce\n`m` further, even when their positions differ. It is not legitimate to\nremove a distinct terminal gradient merely because it is unqueried by the\nlast update; it remains part of this value/normal construction.\n\n## 8. Regularization of an arbitrary 1-smooth original run\n\nReturn now to an arbitrary convex differentiable 1-smooth `f`, with chosen\nminimum zero at zero and initial radius `r>0`. Its original samples are\n`x_i,g_i,f_i`. For `0<c<1`, define `f_c=c f` and run the SAME word on it,\nstarting from the same `x_0`. Denote the new actual positions by `x_i^c`.\nIts smoothness bound is `ell=c<1`, and its minimum set is unchanged.\n\nSet\n\n    M=max_{0<=i<=n}||g_i||,\n    Pi_i=product_{t<i}(1+h_t),\n    B_i=(1-c)M(Pi_i-1).\n\nSubtract the two actual updates and use 1-Lipschitz continuity of `grad f`:\n\n    ||x_{i+1}^c-x_{i+1}||\n      <= (1+c h_i)||x_i^c-x_i||+(1-c)h_i||g_i||\n      <= (1+h_i)||x_i^c-x_i||+(1-c)h_i M.\n\nInduction gives\n\n    ||x_i^c-x_i||<=B_i.                             (8.1)\n\nConvexity and smoothness at the ORIGINAL terminal point imply\n\n    |f(x_n^c)-f_n|<=||g_n||B_n+B_n^2/2.\n\nTherefore the terminal error after scaling the objective is explicitly\n\n    |f_c(x_n^c)-f_n| <= e_c,\n    e_c=(1-c)f_n+c||g_n||B_n+c B_n^2/2 -> 0.        (8.2)\n\nFor example, with `K=f_n+M^2(Pi_n-1)+M^2(Pi_n-1)^2/2`, one has\n`e_c<=(1-c)K`. For a positive original terminal value, `K>0`, so a finite\nchoice of `c<1` sufficiently close to one is immediate from this bound.\n\nThe exact horizontal reproduction in Sections 4--6 is of the ACTUAL\n`f_c` run. There is no assertion that this regularized run is horizontally\nidentical to the original `f` run.\n\nGiven any positive original score and `eta>0`, choose\n\n    0<d<min(f_n,eta r^2/2).\n\nFirst choose `c<1` so that `e_c<d/2`; the new strict-run terminal value is\npositive. Then choose a strict-run loss `0<tau<d/2`, and choose `R,epsilon`\nby (6.7) for THAT strict run. Its total value loss relative to the original\nis less than `d`, so the resulting simplex run has positive terminal value\nand own-closest score\n\n    2 F_D(q_n)/rho^2\n      >= 2(f_n-d)/r^2\n      > 2f_n/r^2-eta.                              (8.3)\n\nThis sequence of choices is important: `c` first, then its finite actual\ntranscript, then `R`, then a positive `epsilon`, then span compression.\nThe parameters may depend on the instance, the entire fixed word, and the\ntolerance. The number of distinct gradient labels may also depend on `c`,\nbut it always satisfies the same bound `m<=p+1`.\n\n## 9. Equality of suprema, scale L, and boundary audit\n\nEvery simplex function in (1.1) is a legal convex differentiable 1-smooth\nfunction with a minimizer, by Section 2. Thus\n\n    W_n^simplex(h)<=W_n(h).\n\nFor every original positive-score instance and every tolerance, (8.3)\nproduces an admissible simplex instance with no more than `p+2` vertices\nand dimension no more than `p+1`. Taking suprema gives the reverse\ninequality and proves the candidate identity (1.1).\n\nOriginal zero-score runs do not require a ratio with zero closest radius.\nThe restricted class is nonempty and has nonnegative scores. It even has\na positive score for every fixed word: in dimension one take `D=[0,1]`\nand start at `q_0=1+S`. Every iterate stays at least one, all projection\ngradients equal one, and the terminal value is `1/2`, with closest radius\n`1+S`. Its score is `1/(1+S)^2>0`.\n\nFor arbitrary `L>0`, replace the original function by\n\n    tilde f(x)=[f(x_*+x)-f(x_*)]/L.\n\nThe normalized step is `h_i`, exactly corresponding to the original\n`h_i/L` step. Multiply the final simplex function by `L`. Its gradient\nis `L P_D`, its algorithm again uses the same word, and its normalized\nscore is still `2F_D(q_n)/rho^2`.\n\nThe complete boundary audit is as follows.\n\n1. **Empty word and all-zero words.** Here `p=0`, positions do not move,\n   there is at most one nonzero gradient label, and `H_i=b_i=0`. A positive\n   score uses a one-dimensional segment simplex. Equations (4.6) and (6.6)\n   reduce respectively to losses `eta_i^2/(2R^2)` and `epsilon^2/2`.\n   The regularization error (8.2) still applies with `Pi_n=1`.\n2. **Zero steps among positive steps.** Duplicate positions and gradients\n   use the same label. Both cumulative vectors simply omit their zero\n   contributions, and no strictness between identical vertices is required.\n3. **Repeated nonzero gradients at different times.** Convexity forces\n   equal heights. Use one vertex and one appended coordinate for the whole\n   label, but retain every physical normal row and its actual `H_i,b_i`.\n   Equation (6.2) is identically zero for a same-label comparison regardless\n   of accumulated time. Different-label comparisons have the explicit bound.\n4. **Zero-gradient samples.** Their function values and heights are zero,\n   and their vertex remains exactly zero. Against nonzero vertices their\n   old rows are strict and their new correction is nonpositive. From such\n   a sample onward the original and lifted runs are stationary. If the\n   terminal score is positive, no zero-gradient sample can have occurred.\n5. **All gradients zero.** The run has score zero. The exact sparse lift\n   is the point body `{0}`. A zero-dimensional simplex convention can\n   describe it, but no own-closest quotient is formed and this case is\n   unnecessary for the positive-score supremum argument.\n6. **Origin/optimum rows.** The reference zero vertex is included in `A`\n   and in every perturbed normal test. Its perturbation coefficient at a\n   nonzero sample is `b_{i,a}+1`, the potentially positive term controlled\n   by `S+1`. It is not dropped. The chosen nearby minimizer is checked\n   separately by its negative inner products with all nonzero vertices.\n7. **Degenerate sparse bodies.** Degeneracy before the orthogonal\n   perturbation causes no issue. Positive `epsilon` makes all nonzero\n   vertices independent. Compression then gives precisely their span,\n   without a full-dimensionality assumption on the original gradient data.\n8. **Huge finite steps and small strict margins.** `A`, `S`, and `Pi_n` may\n   be huge, while `gamma` and `delta` may be tiny. For a fixed finite run\n   they remain finite, with `gamma,delta>0`, so (6.7) permits a finite `R`\n   and a strictly positive `epsilon`. No word-uniform conditioning estimate\n   is asserted, and no limit is interchanged with a supremum or infimum.\n9. **Closest-minimum distance.** Positivity is obtained before dividing;\n   compression preserves the own closest radius exactly by (7.2), while\n   the explicitly chosen minimum gives its upper bound by the original\n   radius. No claim of convergence of these radii is needed.\n\nThe scope remains nonnegative finite steps and finite horizons. Negative\nsteps are not covered by the signs used in `H_i`, `b_i`, and the estimates.\n\n## 10. What this does and does not change\n\nThe first mechanism improves the earlier general-polytope representation\nin a different way: strict smoothness allows finitely many lifted gradient\nPOINTS to satisfy exact projection guards. Its horizontal strict-run\ntrajectory is exact, and its value loss is order `R^{-2}` with explicit\ndata-dependent constants.\n\nThe second mechanism is now supported by a complete quantitative analytical\nargument. It does not assume an equality becomes strict by genericity. All\ngenuine equalities are same-vertex rows, kept exactly zero; every other row\nhas the bound (5.3), and its computed perturbation is controlled by (6.3).\nThe outcome is a simplex with zero as a vertex in dimension at most\n`p+1`, with the complete polar minimum cone still essential.\n\nThe representation retains positive outgoing-optimum slack. In the\npre-compression construction, for `w_i=w^{a(i)}`,\n\n    <Z_i-A_R,w_i>-F_D(Z_i)-||w_i||^2/2\n       =-<A_R,w_i>=eta_i.\n\nAfter compression the same identity holds with `q_i,a`, because their inner\nproducts with every vertex are preserved. It is strictly positive for\nevery nonzero strict-run gradient. Thus this is not an origin-interior or\nan optimum-tight reduction about the nearby minimizer. The simplex has\nzero as a VERTEX and a substantial polar minimum cone.\n\nPointwise equality of the two worst-case formulations allows their infima\nover `h` to be equated after the proof. It does not give an all-word lower\nbound, an improving all-function upper bound, an explicit schedule-only\nadversary, or the required comparison with the exact Zhang--Jiang sequence.\nThat target-level gap is unchanged.\n\n## 11. Provenance, resource accounting, and terminal status\n\n- This is one DISTINCT fresh analytical allocation, following completion\n  of the previous audit; it is not an automatic renewal of that allocation.\n- At most the TWO assigned mechanisms were pursued: sparse normal-guard\n  lifting and quantitative independent-coordinate simplex perturbation.\n- The requested model/effort is `gpt-6-astra/ultra` under the fresh\n  `ALLOCATION.json`; serving-backend metadata is held by the parent dispatch\n  and is not independently exposed within this subagent.\n- Zero mathematical programs, arithmetic replays, solvers, formal verifier\n  calls, fact submissions, paid consults, or subagents were used.\n- Initialization and the current `OPERATOR.md` authorization were re-read.\n  The new allocation and full frozen source were read and hashed. The only\n  accepted mathematical premise used is the interpolation necessity from\n  local fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, already read in the preceding audit and also\n  rederived here at the required smoothness. No external theorem or web\n  source is required.\n- Only public material was used. Only `RESULT_UNVERIFIED.md` and `HANDOFF.md`\n  in this new directory were written. No previous audit artifact, frozen\n  source, service, worker, truth store, or allocation limit was changed.\n\n**Assessment:** no gap found in Mechanism 1; Mechanism 2 closes with the\nexplicit new proof above. This remains a JOINT UNVERIFIED theorem candidate,\nnot an accepted fact. Original exact-profile target: OPEN.\n\n**This ONE new allocation is complete. No further renewal is authorized by\nthis result.**\n\n\n---\n\n# Finite inverse selection with unequal vertex norms\n\nMAIN-AUTHORED UNVERIFIED analytical advisory, not a fact or a new allocation.\nThe native sparse/simplex result is separately reported and remains unverified.\nThe following elementary identity is useful for subsequent geometric search.\n\nLet D=conv{v_0,...,v_k} be any fixed finite convex hull and h>1. Given a\nfuture position y, choose a vertex index s maximizing the finite scores\n\n    score_s=<y,v_s>+(h-1)||v_s||^2/2.\n\nPut x=y+h v_s. For every vertex v_u,\n\n    <x-v_s,v_u-v_s>\n      =score_u-score_s-(h-1)||v_u-v_s||^2/2 <=0.\n\nTherefore P_D(x)=v_s and x-h P_D(x)=y. This is an exact finite vertex\npreimage selector without the equal-norm hypothesis. It applies to the\nactual projection gradient of one static F_D and may be iterated backward\nin the original word when every h_i>1. The zero vertex, if present, is\none ordinary candidate in the score maximum. Ties cause no legality issue.\n\nThis is only a legality construction. It gives NO useful initial-distance\nbound, NO terminal gain and NO original benchmark comparison. Minimization\nof the initial radius among all valid preimages is not implied by this\nmax-score rule. A chosen terminal point and all vertices must still come\nfrom an explicit schedule-selected law before the forward run, and the\nfull polar minimum cone must be used. In particular, do not read the\nsimplex universality theorem candidate as already providing a hard selector.\n\nThe main fully read native27346-byte RESULT_UNVERIFIED.md, SHA256\n29095a40ec7ffc4a27a2a5ada51719542f8b9359ca978b768970dd37eb855f72.\nIts two mechanisms close analytically, but neither has been accepted by\nthe verifier. Four current Danus task allocations are unchanged by this\nadvisory.
